</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Nickname</th><td class="infobox-data">Albo</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1134653256">.mw-parser-output .side-box{margin:4px 0;box-sizing:border-box;border:1px solid #aaa;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em;background-color:#f9f9f9;display:flow-root}.mw-parser-output .side-box-abovebelow,.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{padding:0.25em 0.9em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-image{padding:2px 0 2px 0.9em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-imageright{padding:2px 0.9em 2px 0;text-align:center}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .side-box-flex{display:flex;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{flex:1}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .side-box{width:238px}.mw-parser-output .side-box-right{clear:right;float:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-left{margin-right:1em}}</style><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096940132">.mw-parser-output .listen .side-box-text{line-height:1.1em}.mw-parser-output .listen-plain{border:none;background:transparent}.mw-parser-output .listen-embedded{width:100%;margin:0;border-width:1px 0 0 0;background:transparent}.mw-parser-output .listen-header{padding:2px}.mw-parser-output .listen-embedded .listen-header{padding:2px 0}.mw-parser-output .listen-file-header{padding:4px 0}.mw-parser-output .listen .description{padding-top:2px}.mw-parser-output .listen .mw-tmh-player{max-width:100%}@media(max-width:719px){.mw-parser-output .listen{clear:both}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .listen:not(.listen-noimage){width:320px}.mw-parser-output .listen-left{overflow:visible;float:left}.mw-parser-output .listen-center{float:none;margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto}}</style><div class="side-box side-box-left listen noprint listen-embedded listen-noimage listen-center"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409">

<span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/Government_of_Australia" title="Government of Australia"><img alt="Government of Australia" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg/40px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="31" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg/60px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg/80px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="397" /></a></span></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar" style="text-align:center;"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Anthony_Albanese_sidebar" title="Template:Anthony Albanese sidebar"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Anthony_Albanese_sidebar" title="Template talk:Anthony Albanese sidebar"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Anthony_Albanese_sidebar&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Anthony Norman Albanese</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small> </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">AL</span>-bə-<span style="font-size:90%">NEEZ</span>-ee</i></a> or <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small> </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">AL</span>-bə-neez</i></a>;<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;nb 1&#93;</a></sup> born 2 March 1963) is an Australian politician serving as the 31st and current <a href="/wiki/Prime_minister_of_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime minister of Australia">prime minister of Australia</a> since 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> He has been leader of the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party" title="Australian Labor Party">Australian Labor Party</a> (ALP) since 2019 and the <a href="/wiki/Member_of_parliament" title="Member of parliament">member of parliament</a> (MP) for <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Grayndler" title="Division of Grayndler">Grayndler</a> since 1996. Albanese previously served as the 15th <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Australia" title="Deputy Prime Minister of Australia">deputy prime minister</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Rudd_government_(2013)" title="Rudd government (2013)">second Kevin Rudd government</a> in 2013, and held various other <a href="/wiki/Minister_(government)" title="Minister (government)">ministerial positions</a> in the governments of <a href="/wiki/Kevin_Rudd" title="Kevin Rudd">Kevin Rudd</a> and <a href="/wiki/Julia_Gillard" title="Julia Gillard">Julia Gillard</a> from 2007 to 2013.
</p><p>Albanese was born in <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a> to an Italian father and an <a href="/wiki/Irish-Australian" class="mw-redirect" title="Irish-Australian">Irish-Australian</a> mother who raised him as a <a href="/wiki/Single_parent" title="Single parent">single parent</a>. He attended <a href="/wiki/St_Mary%27s_Cathedral_College,_Sydney" title="St Mary&#39;s Cathedral College, Sydney">St Mary's Cathedral College</a> before going on to the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Sydney" title="University of Sydney">University of Sydney</a> to study economics. He joined the Labor Party as a student, and before entering Parliament worked as a party official and research officer. Albanese was elected to the <a href="/wiki/Australian_House_of_Representatives" title="Australian House of Representatives">House of Representatives</a> at the <a href="/wiki/1996_Australian_federal_election" title="1996 Australian federal election">1996 election</a>, winning the seat of Grayndler in <a href="/wiki/New_South_Wales" title="New South Wales">New South Wales</a>. He was first appointed to the <a href="/wiki/Shadow_cabinet_of_Australia" title="Shadow cabinet of Australia">shadow cabinet</a> in 2001 by <a href="/wiki/Simon_Crean" title="Simon Crean">Simon Crean</a> and went on to serve in a number of roles, eventually becoming <a href="/wiki/Manager_of_Opposition_Business_in_the_House_(Australia)" title="Manager of Opposition Business in the House (Australia)">Manager of Opposition Business</a> in 2006. After Labor's victory in the <a href="/wiki/2007_Australian_federal_election" title="2007 Australian federal election">2007 election</a>, Albanese was appointed <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_(Australia)" title="Leader of the House (Australia)">Leader of the House</a>, and was also made <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Infrastructure_and_Regional_Development_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Infrastructure and Regional Development (Australia)">Minister for Regional Development and Local Government</a> and <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Infrastructure_and_Regional_Development_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Infrastructure and Regional Development (Australia)">Minister for Infrastructure and Transport</a>. In the <a href="/wiki/2010_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill" title="2010 Australian Labor Party leadership spill">subsequent leadership tensions</a> between Kevin Rudd and Julia Gillard from 2010 to 2013, Albanese was publicly critical of the conduct of both, calling for party unity. After supporting Rudd in the final <a href="/wiki/June_2013_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill" title="June 2013 Australian Labor Party leadership spill">leadership ballot</a> between the two in June 2013, Albanese was elected the <a href="/wiki/Leaders_of_the_Australian_Labor_Party" title="Leaders of the Australian Labor Party">deputy leader of the Labor Party</a> and sworn in as deputy prime minister the following day, a position he held for less than three months, as Labor was defeated at the <a href="/wiki/2013_Australian_federal_election" title="2013 Australian federal election">2013 election</a>.
</p><p>After Rudd resigned the leadership and retired from politics, Albanese stood against <a href="/wiki/Bill_Shorten" title="Bill Shorten">Bill Shorten</a> in the <a href="/wiki/October_2013_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_election" title="October 2013 Australian Labor Party leadership election">ensuing leadership election</a>, the first to include party members in addition to MPs. Although Albanese won a large majority of the membership, Shorten won more heavily among Labor MPs and won the contest; Shorten subsequently appointed Albanese to his Shadow Cabinet. After Labor's surprise defeat in the <a href="/wiki/2019_Australian_federal_election" title="2019 Australian federal election">2019 election</a>, Shorten resigned as leader, with Albanese becoming the only person nominated in the <a href="/wiki/2019_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_election" title="2019 Australian Labor Party leadership election">leadership election to replace him</a>; he was subsequently elected unopposed as leader of the Labor Party, becoming <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_(Australia)" title="Leader of the Opposition (Australia)">Leader of the Opposition</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/2022_Australian_federal_election" title="2022 Australian federal election">2022 election</a>, Albanese led his party to a decisive victory against <a href="/wiki/Scott_Morrison" title="Scott Morrison">Scott Morrison</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Liberal-National_Coalition" class="mw-redirect" title="Liberal-National Coalition">Liberal-National Coalition</a>. Albanese is the first <a href="/wiki/Italian-Australian" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian-Australian">Italian-Australian</a> to become prime minister,<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> the first Australian prime minister to have a non-<a href="/wiki/Anglo-Celtic_Australians" title="Anglo-Celtic Australians">Anglo-Celtic</a> surname,<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> and is the last of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_Elizabeth_II#Australia" title="List of prime ministers of Elizabeth II">16 Australian prime ministers</a> who have served under <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Queen Elizabeth II">Queen Elizabeth II</a>. He was sworn in on 23 May 2022, alongside four senior frontbench colleagues.<sup id="cite_ref-swornin3_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-swornin3-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-swornin-abc_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-swornin-abc-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Albanese's first acts as prime minister included updating Australia's climate targets in an effort to reach <a href="/wiki/Carbon_neutrality" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon neutrality">carbon neutrality</a> by 2050, and supporting an increase to the national <a href="/wiki/Minimum_wage" title="Minimum wage">minimum wage</a>. His government legislated a <a href="/wiki/National_Anti-Corruption_Commission_(Australia)" title="National Anti-Corruption Commission (Australia)">national anti-corruption commission</a>, made major changes to <a href="/wiki/Australian_labour_law" title="Australian labour law">Australian labour law</a> and established the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Commission_into_the_Robodebt_Scheme" title="Royal Commission into the Robodebt Scheme">Royal Commission into the Robodebt Scheme</a>. In foreign policy, Albanese pledged further logistical support to Ukraine to assist with the <a href="/wiki/Russo-Ukrainian_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Russo-Ukrainian war">Russo-Ukrainian war</a>, has attempted to strengthen relations in the Pacific region, and held a high-level meeting with Chinese president <a href="/wiki/Xi_Jinping" title="Xi Jinping">Xi Jinping</a>, overseeing an easing of tensions between the countries and leading to easing of trade restrictions put by China on Australia. He also oversaw the official commencement of the <a href="/wiki/AUKUS" title="AUKUS">AUKUS</a> security pact between Australia, the United States, and the United Kingdom.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Family_and_background">Family and background</span></h3>
<p>Albanese was born on 2 March 1963 at <a href="/wiki/St_Margaret%27s_Hospital,_Sydney" title="St Margaret&#39;s Hospital, Sydney">St Margaret's Hospital</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a> suburb of <a href="/wiki/Darlinghurst,_New_South_Wales" class="mw-redirect" title="Darlinghurst, New South Wales">Darlinghurst</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-parlbio2_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parlbio2-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201627_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201627-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> He is the son of Carlo Albanese and Maryanne Ellery.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> His mother was an Australian of Irish descent, while his Italian father was from <a href="/wiki/Barletta" title="Barletta">Barletta</a> in <a href="/wiki/Apulia" title="Apulia">Apulia</a>. His parents met in March 1962 on a voyage from Sydney to <a href="/wiki/Southampton" title="Southampton">Southampton</a>, <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a>, on the <a href="/wiki/Sitmar_Cruises" title="Sitmar Cruises">Sitmar Line</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Fairsky" title="Fairsky">TSS <i>Fairsky</i></a>, where his father worked as a steward, but did not continue their relationship afterwards, going their separate ways.<sup id="cite_ref-middleton2_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-middleton2-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:02_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:12_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Coincidentally, the <i>Fairsky</i> was also the ship on which Albanese's future parliamentary colleague <a href="/wiki/Julia_Gillard" title="Julia Gillard">Julia Gillard</a> and her family migrated to South Australia from the United Kingdom in 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Growing up, Albanese was told that his father had died in a car accident; he did not meet his father, who was in fact still alive, until 2009, tracking him down initially with the assistance of <a href="/wiki/John_Faulkner" title="John Faulkner">John Faulkner</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carnival_Corporation_%26_plc" title="Carnival Corporation &amp; plc">Carnival Australia's</a> CEO <a href="/wiki/Ann_Sherry" title="Ann Sherry">Ann Sherry</a> (the parent company of <a href="/wiki/P%26O_(company)" class="mw-redirect" title="P&amp;O (company)">P&amp;O</a>, which acquired the Sitmar Line in 1988) and maritime historian Rob Henderson, and then later the <a href="/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Australia_to_Italy" title="List of ambassadors of Australia to Italy">Australian Embassy in Italy</a> and ambassador <a href="/wiki/Amanda_Vanstone" title="Amanda Vanstone">Amanda Vanstone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-middleton2_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-middleton2-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He made contact with his father in 2009, visiting him a number of times in Italy, and he took his family there as well. His father died in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He subsequently discovered that he had two half-siblings.<sup id="cite_ref-:02_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:02-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:12_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:12-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Australian_parliamentary_eligibility_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="Australian parliamentary eligibility crisis">Australian parliamentary eligibility crisis</a> of 2017, it was noted that, although birth to an Italian father would ordinarily confer <a href="/wiki/Jus_sanguinis" title="Jus sanguinis">citizenship by descent</a>, Albanese had no father recorded on his birth certificate and thus meets the parliamentary eligibility requirements of <a href="/wiki/Section_44_of_the_Constitution_of_Australia" title="Section 44 of the Constitution of Australia">section 44 of the constitution</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese's maternal grandfather George Ellery ran a printing business on <a href="/wiki/William_Street,_Sydney" title="William Street, Sydney">William Street</a> in Darlinghurst. He provided printing services to the ALP.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201629_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201629-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Childhood_and_education">Childhood and education</span></h3>
<p>Albanese grew up with his mother and maternal grandparents in a <a href="/wiki/Sydney_City_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Sydney City Council">Sydney City Council</a> home in the <a href="/wiki/Inner_West" title="Inner West">Inner West</a> suburb of <a href="/wiki/Camperdown,_New_South_Wales" title="Camperdown, New South Wales">Camperdown</a>, opposite the <a href="/wiki/Camperdown_Children%27s_Hospital" class="mw-redirect" title="Camperdown Children&#39;s Hospital">Camperdown Children's Hospital</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201642_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201642-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> His grandfather died in 1970, and the following year his mother married James Williamson. He was given his stepfather's surname, but the marriage lasted only 10 weeks, as Williamson proved to be an abusive alcoholic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201636-38_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201636-38-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Albanese's mother worked part-time as a cleaner but suffered from chronic <a href="/wiki/Rheumatoid_arthritis" title="Rheumatoid arthritis">rheumatoid arthritis</a>, with the family surviving on her disability pension and his grandmother's age pension.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201638_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201638-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese attended St Joseph's Primary School in Camperdown<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201644_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton201644-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> and then <a href="/wiki/St_Mary%27s_Cathedral_College,_Sydney" title="St Mary&#39;s Cathedral College, Sydney">St Mary's Cathedral College</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ALP_website_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALP_website-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> After finishing school, he worked for the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Bank" title="Commonwealth Bank">Commonwealth Bank</a> for two years before studying economics at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Sydney" title="University of Sydney">University of Sydney</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-parlbio2_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parlbio2-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> There, he became involved in student politics and was elected to the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Sydney_Students%27_Representative_Council" title="University of Sydney Students&#39; Representative Council">Students' Representative Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-student_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-student-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> It was also there where he started his rise as a key player in the ALP's <a href="/wiki/Labor_Left" title="Labor Left">Labor Left</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-crikey_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crikey-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> During his time in student politics, Albanese led a group within Young Labor that was aligned with the left faction's Hard Left, which maintained "links with broader left-wing groups, such as the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Australia_(1971)" title="Communist Party of Australia (1971)">Communist Party of Australia</a>, People for Nuclear Disarmament and the <a href="/wiki/African_National_Congress" title="African National Congress">African National Congress</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-factions&amp;fractions_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factions&amp;fractions-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese's mother died in 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-parliamentary_career_and_travel">Pre-parliamentary career and travel</span></h3>
<p>After completing his economics degree in 1984,<sup id="cite_ref-cityhub_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cityhub-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Albanese took on a role as a research officer to the then <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Local_Government,_Territories_and_Roads" title="Minister for Local Government, Territories and Roads">Minister for Local Government and</a> <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Finance_(Australia)" title="Minister for Finance (Australia)">Administrative Services</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tom_Uren" title="Tom Uren">Tom Uren</a>, who became a mentor to him.<sup id="cite_ref-powerindex_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-powerindex-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In 1989, the position of Assistant General Secretary of the New South Wales branch of the Labor Party became vacant when <a href="/wiki/John_Faulkner" title="John Faulkner">John Faulkner</a> was elected to the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Senate" title="Australian Senate">Senate</a>. The election to replace him was closely disputed between the Labor Left's Hard Left and Soft Left groupings, with Albanese being elected with the backing of the Hard Left, taking on that role for the next six years.<sup id="cite_ref-factions&amp;fractions_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-factions&amp;fractions-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> In 1995, he left the position to work as a senior adviser to <a href="/wiki/Premier_of_New_South_Wales" title="Premier of New South Wales">New South Wales Premier</a> <a href="/wiki/Bob_Carr" title="Bob Carr">Bob Carr</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-parlbio2_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parlbio2-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese's first overseas trip was in 1986, accompanying his friend <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Fisher_(author)" title="Jeremy Fisher (author)">Jeremy Fisher</a> to <a href="/wiki/Vanuatu" title="Vanuatu">Vanuatu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016139_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016139-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> In 1987, Albanese joined his boss Tom Uren on a visit to South-East Asia, which included: a meeting of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Economic_and_Social_Commission_for_Asia_and_the_Pacific" title="United Nations Economic and Social Commission for Asia and the Pacific">United Nations Economic and Social Commission for Asia and the Pacific</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bangkok" title="Bangkok">Bangkok</a>, Thailand; an <a href="/wiki/Dawn_service" class="mw-redirect" title="Dawn service">Anzac Day dawn service</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Kanchanaburi_War_Cemetery" title="Kanchanaburi War Cemetery">Kanchanaburi War Cemetery</a> with <a href="/wiki/John_Carrick_(Australian_politician)" title="John Carrick (Australian politician)">John Carrick</a>; and a tour of <a href="/wiki/Cambodia" title="Cambodia">Cambodia</a> alongside <a href="/wiki/Bill_Hayden" title="Bill Hayden">Bill Hayden</a>'s daughter Ingrid.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016140-43_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016140-43-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> He then travelled extensively in 1988, visiting <a href="/wiki/Zimbabwe" title="Zimbabwe">Zimbabwe</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zambia" title="Zambia">Zambia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Botswana" title="Botswana">Botswana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Western_Europe" title="Western Europe">Western Europe</a> on a <a href="/wiki/Contiki_Tours" title="Contiki Tours">Contiki tour</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern Europe</a> and <a href="/wiki/Scandinavia" title="Scandinavia">Scandinavia</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Backpacking_(travel)" title="Backpacking (travel)">backpacker</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016181-84_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016181-84-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Upon returning to Australia, he began dating <a href="/wiki/Carmel_Tebbutt" title="Carmel Tebbutt">Carmel Tebbutt</a>, with whom he would holiday in Europe and South-East Asia,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016191_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016191-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> plus a backpacking trip to <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> in 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Sometime during his 20s, Albanese also took part in a tour of the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> organised by the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">State Department</a>, with a thematic focus on the interaction of <a href="/wiki/Advocacy_group" title="Advocacy group">advocacy groups</a> with the U.S. Government.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1990, Albanese bought a <a href="/wiki/Semi-detached" title="Semi-detached">semi-detached</a> two-bedroom house in the Inner West Sydney suburb of <a href="/wiki/Marrickville,_New_South_Wales" title="Marrickville, New South Wales">Marrickville</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cityhub_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cityhub-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_political_career">Early political career</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Entry_to_Parliament">Entry to Parliament</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_Albanese_1990s.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Anthony_Albanese_1990s.jpg/170px-Anthony_Albanese_1990s.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="213" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/82/Anthony_Albanese_1990s.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="200" data-file-height="250" /></a><figcaption>Albanese shortly after his election to Parliament</figcaption></figure>
<p>When <a href="/wiki/Jeannette_McHugh" title="Jeannette McHugh">Jeannette McHugh</a> announced she would not seek re-election in her seat of <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Grayndler" title="Division of Grayndler">Grayndler</a> at the <a href="/wiki/1996_Australian_federal_election" title="1996 Australian federal election">1996 election</a>, Albanese won preselection for the seat. The campaign was a difficult one, with aircraft noise a big political issue following the opening of the third runway at <a href="/wiki/Sydney_Airport" title="Sydney Airport">Sydney Airport</a>, and the newly established <a href="/wiki/No_Aircraft_Noise" title="No Aircraft Noise">No Aircraft Noise</a> party (NAN) having polled strongly in the local area at the <a href="/wiki/1995_New_South_Wales_state_election" title="1995 New South Wales state election">1995 New South Wales election</a>. Veteran political pundit <a href="/wiki/Malcolm_Mackerras" title="Malcolm Mackerras">Malcolm Mackerras</a> predicted NAN would win the seat. However, NAN's candidate finished third, with less than 14% of the vote. Despite suffering a six-point swing against Labor, Albanese was elected with a comfortable 16-point margin.<sup id="cite_ref-maiden_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maiden-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his maiden speech to the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Representatives (Australia)">House of Representatives</a>, he spoke about the building of a third runway at <a href="/wiki/Sydney_Airport" title="Sydney Airport">Sydney Airport</a>, aircraft noise and the need to build a second airport to service Sydney, as well as his support for funding public infrastructure in general, <a href="/wiki/Multiculturalism" title="Multiculturalism">multiculturalism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Native_title" class="mw-redirect" title="Native title">native title</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Social_wage" class="mw-redirect" title="Social wage">social wage</a> and childcare. He concluded by saying, "For myself, I will be satisfied if I can be remembered as someone who will stand up for the interests of my electorate, for working-class people, for the labour movement, and for our progressive advancement as a nation into the next century."<sup id="cite_ref-maiden_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maiden-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his first year in Parliament he continued this theme, speaking in favour of the <a href="/wiki/Rights_of_the_Terminally_Ill_Act_1995" title="Rights of the Terminally Ill Act 1995">Northern Territory's euthanasia legislation</a>, the rights of the Indigenous community in the <a href="/wiki/Hindmarsh_Island_bridge_controversy" title="Hindmarsh Island bridge controversy">Hindmarsh Island bridge controversy</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> and entitlement to <a href="/wiki/Superannuation_in_Australia" title="Superannuation in Australia">superannuation</a> for same-sex couples.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This latter issue became a cause to which he was particularly dedicated. In 1998 he unsuccessfully moved a private member's bill that would have given same-sex couples the same rights to superannuation as de facto heterosexual couples.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Over the next nine years, he tried three more times without success, until the election of the Rudd government in 2007 saw the legislation passed.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Albanese subsequently turned his attention to campaigning for same-sex marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Appointment_to_Shadow_Cabinet">Appointment to Shadow Cabinet</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_Albanese,_Australian_Labor_MP_in_2005.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Anthony_Albanese%2C_Australian_Labor_MP_in_2005.jpg/220px-Anthony_Albanese%2C_Australian_Labor_MP_in_2005.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/cc/Anthony_Albanese%2C_Australian_Labor_MP_in_2005.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="288" data-file-height="384" /></a><figcaption>Albanese in 2005</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1998, Albanese was appointed a parliamentary secretary, a position which assists ministers and shadow ministers and is often a stepping stone to a full ministerial position.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2001, he was promoted to the opposition <a href="/wiki/Shadow_Cabinet" class="mw-redirect" title="Shadow Cabinet">Shadow Cabinet</a>, taking the portfolio of ageing and seniors. A 2002 reshuffle saw him become Shadow Minister for Employment Services and Training, and in 2004 he became Shadow Minister for Environment and Heritage.<sup id="cite_ref-parlbio2_13-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parlbio2-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> It was during this latter role that then prime minister <a href="/wiki/John_Howard" title="John Howard">John Howard</a> and science minister <a href="/wiki/Brendan_Nelson" title="Brendan Nelson">Brendan Nelson</a> started raising the idea of <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_Australia" title="Nuclear power in Australia">nuclear power for Australia</a>. Albanese campaigned strongly against them, as well as elements within his own party, arguing that "Nuclear energy doesn't add up economically, environmentally or socially, and after more than 50 years of debate, we still do not have an answer to nuclear proliferation or nuclear waste."<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2005, he was given the additional role of Shadow Minister for Water alongside his existing responsibilities, and was also appointed Deputy Manager of Opposition Business in the House. In December 2006, when <a href="/wiki/Kevin_Rudd" title="Kevin Rudd">Kevin Rudd</a> first became Leader of the Labor Party, Albanese took over from <a href="/wiki/Julia_Gillard" title="Julia Gillard">Julia Gillard</a> as <a href="/wiki/Manager_of_Opposition_Business_in_the_House_(Australia)" title="Manager of Opposition Business in the House (Australia)">Manager of Opposition Business in the House</a>, a senior tactical role on the floor of the parliament, and was appointed Shadow Minister for Water and Infrastructure.<sup id="cite_ref-parlbio2_13-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-parlbio2-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cabinet_minister">Cabinet minister</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_Albanese.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Anthony_Albanese.jpg/220px-Anthony_Albanese.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="314" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Anthony_Albanese.jpg/330px-Anthony_Albanese.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Anthony_Albanese.jpg/440px-Anthony_Albanese.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2239" data-file-height="3193" /></a><figcaption>Albanese in 2011</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Rudd_government">Rudd government</span></h3>
<p>Following Labor's victory at the <a href="/wiki/2007_Australian_federal_election" title="2007 Australian federal election">2007 election</a>, Albanese's rise in standing within the party was evidenced by his appointment as <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Infrastructure_and_Regional_Development_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Infrastructure and Regional Development (Australia)">Minister for Infrastructure and Transport</a>, <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Infrastructure_and_Regional_Development_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister for Infrastructure and Regional Development (Australia)">Minister for Regional Development and Local Government</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_(Australia)" title="Leader of the House (Australia)">Leader of the House of Representatives</a> in the <a href="/wiki/First_Rudd_Ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="First Rudd Ministry">Rudd ministry</a>. Rudd was sworn in alongside his colleagues on 3 December 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Labor Party had gone to the election criticising the previous government for ignoring "long-term nation building in favour of short-term political spending".<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> One of Albanese's first moves as Minister for Infrastructure and Transport was the establishment of an independent statutory body, <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure_Australia" title="Infrastructure Australia">Infrastructure Australia</a>, to advise the Government on infrastructure priorities. Armed with advice from this independent body and his own persuasive skills in the Cabinet, he was able to argue for a doubling of the roads budget and a tenfold increase in rail investment.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The establishment of Infrastructure Australia was regarded by many as a success; projects delivered through the Infrastructure Australia process included Melbourne's <a href="/wiki/Regional_Rail_Link" class="mw-redirect" title="Regional Rail Link">Regional Rail Link</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Hunter_Expressway" title="Hunter Expressway">Hunter Expressway</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Ipswich_Motorway" title="Ipswich Motorway">Ipswich Motorway</a>, the Gold Coast light rail system <a href="/wiki/G:link" title="G:link">G:link</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Redcliffe_Peninsula_railway_line" title="Redcliffe Peninsula railway line">Redcliffe Peninsula railway line</a>, the extension of the <a href="/wiki/Seaford_railway_line" class="mw-redirect" title="Seaford railway line">Noarlunga Centre railway line</a> to <a href="/wiki/Seaford,_South_Australia" title="Seaford, South Australia">Seaford, South Australia</a> and various projects along the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Motorway_(Sydney%E2%80%93Newcastle)" title="Pacific Motorway (Sydney–Newcastle)">Pacific Highway</a> in NSW and <a href="/wiki/Bruce_Highway" title="Bruce Highway">Bruce Highway</a> in Queensland.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gillard_government">Gillard government</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_Albanese_2013_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="238" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Anthony_Albanese_2013_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="733" data-file-height="1026" /></a><figcaption>Albanese at the opening of the Holbrook Bypass in 2013</figcaption></figure>
<p>After <a href="/wiki/Julia_Gillard" title="Julia Gillard">Julia Gillard</a> replaced Rudd as prime minister following the <a href="/wiki/2010_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill" title="2010 Australian Labor Party leadership spill">leadership spill</a> in June 2010 she retained Albanese in his roles.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Following the <a href="/wiki/2010_Australian_federal_election" title="2010 Australian federal election">2010 election</a>, which resulted in a <a href="/wiki/Hung_parliament" title="Hung parliament">hung parliament</a>, Albanese was a key player in negotiating the support of independent members <a href="/wiki/Tony_Windsor" title="Tony Windsor">Tony Windsor</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rob_Oakeshott" title="Rob Oakeshott">Rob Oakeshott</a> through his role of Leader of the House. Albanese was also responsible for managing legislation through the House in the first hung parliament since the 1940s.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011, Albanese introduced two more major policy reforms. The first on urban planning drew on the work of Danish designer <a href="/wiki/Jan_Gehl" title="Jan Gehl">Jan Gehl</a> and set out plans for urban design with better transport links and safety.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The second, on shipping, was notable for gaining the approval of both the conservative Australian Shipowners Associations and the radical <a href="/wiki/Maritime_Union_of_Australia" title="Maritime Union of Australia">Maritime Union of Australia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> However, he also attracted controversy when a convoy of trucks from North Queensland dubbed the "convoy of no confidence" descended on Canberra's Parliament House to protest against rising fuel costs and <a href="/wiki/Carbon_pricing" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon pricing">carbon pricing</a>. During question time, Albanese labelled the protesters outside as "the convoy of no consequence". This caused outrage among supporters of the protest and a week later a public rally in support of the truckies was held outside Albanese's electorate office in <a href="/wiki/Marrickville,_New_South_Wales" title="Marrickville, New South Wales">Marrickville</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_South_Wales" title="New South Wales">New South Wales</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Face-off_part_II_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Face-off_part_II-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following a series of poor polls, leadership instability descended again on the Labor government. Former prime minister Kevin Rudd resigned as <a href="/wiki/Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs_(Australia)" title="Minister for Foreign Affairs (Australia)">Minister for Foreign Affairs</a> in February 2012 to <a href="/wiki/2012_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill" title="2012 Australian Labor Party leadership spill">unsuccessfully challenge</a> Julia Gillard for the leadership.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Shortly before the ballot, Albanese came out in support of Rudd, stating that he had always been unhappy with the manner of Rudd's removal.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> He tearfully explained how he had offered his resignation as Leader of the House to the prime minister, but that she had refused to accept it, and called on Labor to cease leadership divisions and unify. In response to a question on his personal feelings around the leadership spill, he stated "I like fighting Tories. That's what I do."<sup id="cite_ref-ANN_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ANN-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Australia">Deputy Prime Minister of Australia</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg/220px-Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg/330px-Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg/440px-Anthony_Albanese_swearing-in_2013.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2362" data-file-height="1572" /></a><figcaption>Albanese sworn in as deputy prime minister by <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia" title="Governor-General of Australia">Governor-General</a> <a href="/wiki/Quentin_Bryce" title="Quentin Bryce">Quentin Bryce</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>In June 2013, Rudd defeated Gillard in a final <a href="/wiki/June_2013_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_spill" title="June 2013 Australian Labor Party leadership spill">leadership election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> That same ballot saw Albanese elected by the caucus as <a href="/wiki/Leaders_of_the_Australian_Labor_Party#ALP_federal_deputy_parliamentary_leaders" title="Leaders of the Australian Labor Party">Deputy Leader of the Labor Party</a>, and the following day Albanese was sworn in as <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Australia" title="Deputy Prime Minister of Australia">deputy prime minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> He held this role until Labor's defeat at the <a href="/wiki/2013_Australian_federal_election" title="2013 Australian federal election">2013 election</a>, and was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Warren_Truss" title="Warren Truss">Warren Truss</a> on 18 September.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_to_Opposition">Return to Opposition</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2013_leadership_election">2013 leadership election</span></h3>
<p>Following the defeat of Labor at the <a href="/wiki/2013_Australian_federal_election" title="2013 Australian federal election">2013 election</a>, Albanese announced his candidacy to be <a href="/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party#ALP_federal_parliamentary_leaders" title="Australian Labor Party">Leader of the Labor Party</a>, standing against <a href="/wiki/Bill_Shorten" title="Bill Shorten">Bill Shorten</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Shorten was announced as the winner after a month-long contest that was the first to involve a combined vote of MPs and rank-and-file members. Although Albanese won comfortably among party members, Shorten held a greater lead among MPs, and was subsequently elected.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Shorten_Opposition">Shorten Opposition</span></h3>
<p>In October 2013, shortly after the leadership election, Shorten appointed Albanese Shadow Minister for Infrastructure and Transport and Shadow Minister for Tourism; he held these roles throughout Shorten's time as leader.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> In September 2014, Albanese was given the additional role of Shadow Minister for Cities.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Leader_of_the_Opposition_.282019.E2.80.932022.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Leader_of_the_Opposition_(2019–2022)">Leader of the Opposition (2019–2022)</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_(cropped_-_tight).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg/170px-Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg/255px-Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg/340px-Australian_Labor_Party_Leader_Anthony_Albanese_MP_%28cropped_-_tight%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1089" data-file-height="1633" /></a><figcaption>Albanese during his time as Leader of the Opposition</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2019_leadership_election">2019 leadership election</span></h3>
<p>Bill Shorten announced his resignation as Leader of the Labor Party on 18 May 2019, following Labor's unexpected defeat in the <a href="/wiki/2019_Australian_federal_election" title="2019 Australian federal election">2019 election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-48304993_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-48304993-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> The day after, Albanese announced his candidacy in the subsequent <a href="/wiki/2019_Australian_Labor_Party_leadership_election" title="2019 Australian Labor Party leadership election">leadership election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> On 21 May, <a href="/wiki/Chris_Bowen" title="Chris Bowen">Chris Bowen</a> announced he would also contest the ballot; however, the next day, he announced his withdrawal, citing his lack of support among the party membership.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> With no other candidate stepping forward, Albanese took the leadership unopposed on 30 May, with <a href="/wiki/Richard_Marles" title="Richard Marles">Richard Marles</a> as his deputy.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Aged 56 when he took office, he became the oldest first-time Opposition Leader in 59 years, since <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Calwell" title="Arthur Calwell">Arthur Calwell</a> (aged 63) took office in 1960.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Albanese unveiled his <a href="/wiki/Shadow_Ministry_of_Anthony_Albanese" title="Shadow Ministry of Anthony Albanese">shadow ministry</a> on 1 June 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2022_federal_election">2022 federal election</span></h3>
<p>Albanese led the Labor Party into the <a href="/wiki/2022_Australian_federal_election" title="2022 Australian federal election">2022 federal election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> On the first day of campaigning, Albanese was unable to name either the <a href="/wiki/Official_cash_rate" title="Official cash rate">official cash rate</a> or unemployment rate, which drew criticism.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> On 20 April, Albanese faced prime minister <a href="/wiki/Scott_Morrison" title="Scott Morrison">Scott Morrison</a> in a debate hosted by <a href="/wiki/Sky_News_Australia" title="Sky News Australia">Sky News</a>, with Albanese being deemed the winner through an audience vote.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> However, the next day, Albanese tested positive for <a href="/wiki/COVID-19" title="COVID-19">COVID-19</a>, forcing him to isolate at home in Sydney.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> He returned to campaigning the following week and, on 1 May, hosted Labor's campaign launch in Perth which was the first time any major party launched in Western Australia. At the launch, Labor unveiled policies to reduce the cost of medicine and childcare, increase <a href="/wiki/Manufacturing_in_Australia" title="Manufacturing in Australia">manufacturing in Australia</a>, and introduce a <a href="/wiki/Equity_sharing" title="Equity sharing">shared equity housing scheme</a> to assist first-time home buyers.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Albanese faced Morrison in two further debates, hosted by <a href="/wiki/Nine_Network" title="Nine Network">Channel Nine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Seven_Network" title="Seven Network">Channel Seven</a>, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Opinion_polls" class="mw-redirect" title="Opinion polls">Opinion polling</a> indicated that support for the two major parties would reach record lows, due to high levels of support for third parties and independent candidates.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> However, unlike his predecessor, Albanese was able to ultimately able to lead the Labor Party to federal victory, defeating Prime Minister Scott Morrison and his government with a one-seat majority in the House of Representatives.
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_Minister_of_Australia_.282022.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_of_Australia_(2022–present)">Prime Minister of Australia (2022–present)</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Albanese_government" title="Albanese government">Albanese government</a> and <a href="/wiki/Albanese_ministry" title="Albanese ministry">Albanese ministry</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/84px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="290" /></span></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article needs to be <b>updated</b>. The reason given is: Missing information on 2023 budget.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">May 2023</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg/250px-V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg/375px-V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg/500px-V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng_and_Anthony_Albanese.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1063" data-file-height="653" /></a><figcaption>Albanese with Vietnamese President <a href="/wiki/V%C3%B5_V%C4%83n_Th%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng" title="Võ Văn Thưởng">Võ Văn Thưởng</a> in Hanoi, Vietnam, June 2023</figcaption></figure>
<p>Labor was victorious over the incumbent <a href="/wiki/Coalition_(Australia)" title="Coalition (Australia)">Liberal-National Coalition</a> at the federal election on 21 May 2022, with Albanese becoming the 31st prime minister of Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Despite a fall in the party's primary vote, Labor won a number of seats from the Coalition, helped by a particularly large swing to the party in <a href="/wiki/Western_Australia" title="Western Australia">Western Australia</a>; the result was also assisted by a number of "<a href="/wiki/Teal_independents" title="Teal independents">teal independents</a>" winning seats from <a href="/wiki/Moderates_(Liberal_Party_of_Australia)" title="Moderates (Liberal Party of Australia)">"moderate" Liberal members</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although it was not certain that Labor would win a majority, it soon became apparent that no other party could realistically form a government.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Accordingly, two days after the election, Albanese, deputy leader <a href="/wiki/Richard_Marles" title="Richard Marles">Richard Marles</a>, former shadow treasurer <a href="/wiki/Jim_Chalmers" title="Jim Chalmers">Jim Chalmers</a>, and senators <a href="/wiki/Penny_Wong" title="Penny Wong">Penny Wong</a> and <a href="/wiki/Katy_Gallagher" title="Katy Gallagher">Katy Gallagher</a> were sworn in as an interim five-person ministry. According to Australia's <a href="/wiki/ABC_News_(Australia)" title="ABC News (Australia)">ABC News</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia" title="Governor-General of Australia">Governor-General</a>, <a href="/wiki/David_Hurley" title="David Hurley">David Hurley</a>, would not have sworn in Albanese without assurances that Labor could provide stable government, as well as legal advice that this was the proper course of action.<sup id="cite_ref-swornin-abc_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-swornin-abc-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Albanese is the first <a href="/wiki/Italian-Australian" class="mw-redirect" title="Italian-Australian">Italian-Australian</a> prime minister in the country's history.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese secured <a href="/wiki/Confidence_and_supply" title="Confidence and supply">confidence and supply</a> from several <a href="/wiki/Crossbencher" title="Crossbencher">crossbenchers</a> in the event that he was unable to form <a href="/wiki/Majority_government" title="Majority government">majority government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> However, on 30 May, it was projected that Labor had won at least 76 seats, enough to win a majority for the first time at the federal level since the <a href="/wiki/2007_Australian_federal_election" title="2007 Australian federal election">2007 election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Albanese_ministry" title="Albanese ministry">Albanese's full ministry</a> was sworn in on 1 June.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Domestic_affairs">Domestic affairs</span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate_change">Climate change</span></h4>
<p>On 16 June 2022, Albanese submitted a new <a href="/wiki/Nationally_determined_contribution" title="Nationally determined contribution">Nationally Determined Contribution</a> to the United Nations which formally committed Australia to reducing <a href="/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions_by_Australia" title="Greenhouse gas emissions by Australia">carbon emissions</a> by 43% on 2005 levels. This represented an increase from the 26 to 28% target under the previous government.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> In September 2022, the Albanese government passed legislation to write this climate target into law.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Albanese's government also entered a bid for Australia and its Pacific island neighbours to host the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Climate_Change_conference" title="United Nations Climate Change conference">2024 United Nations Climate Change conference</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In late-2022, his government announced reforms to the "safeguard mechanism", which requires Australia's largest carbon emitters to keep their emissions under a "baseline limit", either by reducing them, or by purchasing <a href="/wiki/Carbon_credit" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon credit">carbon credits</a>. The mechanism was introduced by the <a href="/wiki/Turnbull_government" title="Turnbull government">Turnbull government</a> in 2016, but failed to reduce emissions as the rules were often left unenforced.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> In March 2023, the Albanese government received the necessary support to pass the legislation from the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Greens" title="Australian Greens">Greens</a>, who negotiated a "hard cap" on emissions that cannot be offset by carbon credits.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> The bill was passed on 30 March 2023, marking the most significant piece of climate change legislation passed through the Australian parliament since the <i><a href="/wiki/Clean_Energy_Act_2011" title="Clean Energy Act 2011">Clean Energy Act 2011</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Minimum_wage">Minimum wage</span></h4>
<p>On 27 May 2022, Albanese sent correspondence to the <a href="/wiki/Fair_Work_Commission" title="Fair Work Commission">Fair Work Commission</a> confirming that his government would seek to make a submission to the Commission in support of an increase to the minimum wage.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The government announced that a submission had been formally made to the commission on 3 June 2022 and that a "deliberate" policy of lower wages was not the policy of the new government.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> The Fair Work Commission subsequently announced on 15 June 2022 that the minimum wage would be raised by 5.2%.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="National_anti-corruption_commission">National anti-corruption commission</span></h4>
<p>During the 2022 election campaign, Albanese pledged to establish a <a href="/wiki/National_Anti-Corruption_Commission_(Australia)" title="National Anti-Corruption Commission (Australia)">national anti-corruption commission</a>, saying that it would be one of his "first priorities".<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> On 27 September, attorney-general <a href="/wiki/Mark_Dreyfus" title="Mark Dreyfus">Mark Dreyfus</a> introduced the relevant legislation to Parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The bill passed on 30 November 2022 in line with Albanese's commitment to legislate an anti-corruption commission prior to the end of the calendar year.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Indigenous_affairs">Indigenous affairs</span></h4>
<p>In his victory speech, Albanese expressed his support for the <i><a href="/wiki/Uluru_Statement_from_the_Heart" title="Uluru Statement from the Heart">Uluru Statement from the Heart</a></i>, and stated that his government would implement it in full within its first term.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> He has pledged to hold a referendum, which if successful, will enshrine an <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_Voice_to_Parliament" title="Indigenous Voice to Parliament">Indigenous Voice to Parliament</a> to assist the government with Indigenous issues, and <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_recognition_of_Indigenous_Australians" title="Constitutional recognition of Indigenous Australians">recognise</a> Indigenous Australians in <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Australia" title="Constitution of Australia">Australia's constitution</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/2023_Australian_Indigenous_Voice_referendum" title="2023 Australian Indigenous Voice referendum">referendum</a> is expected to be held in late 2023.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Industrial_relations">Industrial relations</span></h4>
<p>The government passed new workplace harassment laws through the Parliament on 28 November 2022. The news laws are in line with Albanese's promise to implement the recommendations of the Respect@Work Report by creating a positive duty requiring employers to implement measures to prevent sexual harassment.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 2 December 2022, the government's <i>Secure Jobs, Better Pay</i> bill passed the Parliament. Under the new laws, unions can now negotiate multi-employer pay deals in an effort to secure wage increases across particular sectors such as child care and aged care. The law also aims to close the gender pay gap by prohibiting pay secrecy employment clauses and secures the right of workers to seek flexible working arrangements.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_affairs">International affairs</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo,_in_Tokyo,_Japan_on_May_24,_2022.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg/241px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg" decoding="async" width="241" height="178" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg/362px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg/482px-The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_at_the_QUAD_Leaders%E2%80%99_Family_Photo%2C_in_Tokyo%2C_Japan_on_May_24%2C_2022.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1622" /></a><figcaption>On the same day he was sworn in as prime minister, Albanese attended his first overseas trip: the <a href="/wiki/Quadrilateral_Security_Dialogue" title="Quadrilateral Security Dialogue">Quadrilateral Security Dialogue</a> meeting with US president <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a>, Indian prime minister <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> and Japanese prime minister <a href="/wiki/Fumio_Kishida" title="Fumio Kishida">Fumio Kishida</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Albanese took his first international trip on 23 May 2022 immediately after being sworn in as prime minister when he flew to Tokyo to attend a <a href="/wiki/Quadrilateral_Security_Dialogue" title="Quadrilateral Security Dialogue">Quadrilateral Security Dialogue</a> meeting with fellow world leaders: US president <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a>, Indian prime minister <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> and Japanese prime minister <a href="/wiki/Fumio_Kishida" title="Fumio Kishida">Fumio Kishida</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> At the meeting, Albanese committed his new government to the goals of the Quad and confirmed that his government would seek to take stronger action in reducing carbon emissions.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> On 5 June, Albanese and <a href="/wiki/Penny_Wong" title="Penny Wong">Penny Wong</a> visited Indonesian president <a href="/wiki/Joko_Widodo" title="Joko Widodo">Joko Widodo</a> in <a href="/wiki/Jakarta" title="Jakarta">Jakarta</a> to develop <a href="/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Indonesia_relations" title="Australia–Indonesia relations">Australia–Indonesia relations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Albanese said he would not "publicly intervene" to prevent <a href="/wiki/WikiLeaks" title="WikiLeaks">WikiLeaks</a> founder <a href="/wiki/Julian_Assange" title="Julian Assange">Julian Assange</a> from being extradited to the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a
