2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2001" /></a><figcaption>Albanese visiting Ukrainian president <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a> in Kyiv</figcaption></figure>
<p>Later in June, Albanese attended the <a href="/wiki/2022_NATO_Madrid_summit" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 NATO Madrid summit">2022 NATO Madrid summit</a> to discuss security threats facing the Pacific region.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> On 30 June, Albanese met with French president <a href="/wiki/Emmanuel_Macron" title="Emmanuel Macron">Emmanuel Macron</a> in Paris to "reset" <a href="/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93France_relations" title="Australia–France relations">Australia–France relations</a>, which had been damaged following the cancellation of a <a href="/wiki/Attack-class_submarine" title="Attack-class submarine">submarine deal</a> by the preceding government.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> The next day, Albanese travelled to Ukraine to meet with president <a href="/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy" title="Volodymyr Zelenskyy">Volodymyr Zelenskyy</a>, making him the first Australian prime minister to make a diplomatic visit to Ukraine.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Albanese pledged a further $100 million in aid to assist with the ongoing <a href="/wiki/Russo-Ukrainian_War" title="Russo-Ukrainian War">Russo-Ukrainian War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 September 2022, Albanese travelled to Japan to attend the state funeral of former prime minister <a href="/wiki/Shinzo_Abe" title="Shinzo Abe">Shinzo Abe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Joe_Biden,_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego,_California,_March_13,_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0319.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0319.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0319.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/President_Joe_Biden%2C_British_Prime_Minister_Rishi_Sunak_and_Australian_Prime_Minister_Anthony_Albanese_at_the_AUKUS_meeting_in_San_Diego%2C_California%2C_March_13%2C_2023_-_230313-D-TT977-0319.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7047" data-file-height="4698" /></a><figcaption>Albanese (far left) at an <a href="/wiki/AUKUS" title="AUKUS">AUKUS</a> summit in March 2023</figcaption></figure>
<p>The relationship between Australia and China started to improve since Albanese became prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In November 2022, Albanese held a bilateral meeting with Chinese leader <a href="/wiki/Xi_Jinping" title="Xi Jinping">Xi Jinping</a>, bringing an end to the longest diplomatic freeze in 50 years between Australia and China.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> In early 2023, China ended its unofficial ban on imports of Australian coal,<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> with all restrictions reportedly being lifted by 14 March.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> China agreed to lift its ban on barley imports from Australia in April,<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> and imports of Australian timber in May, further improving the relations.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2023, Albanese hosted his <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_New_Zealand" title="Prime Minister of New Zealand">New Zealand counterpart</a> <a href="/wiki/Chris_Hipkins" title="Chris Hipkins">Chris Hipkins</a>, who undertook his first state visit. While the two leaders reaffirmed <a href="/wiki/Australia-New_Zealand_relations" class="mw-redirect" title="Australia-New Zealand relations">Australian-New Zealand bilateral relations</a>, they also discussed the controversial <a href="/wiki/Migration_Act_1958" title="Migration Act 1958">Section 501 deportation policy</a>. Albanese confirmed that <a href="/wiki/Albanese_government" title="Albanese government">his government</a> would amend the deportation policy to take into account individuals' connections to Australia and the length of time they had lived in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia,_Anthony_Albanese_(52909315146).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg/220px-19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg/330px-19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg/440px-19.05.2023_-_Encontro_com_o_Primeiro-Ministro_da_Comunidade_da_Austr%C3%A1lia%2C_Anthony_Albanese_%2852909315146%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3984" data-file-height="2656" /></a><figcaption>Albanese (left) with Brazilian president <a href="/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva" title="Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva">Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva</a> (right) at the <a href="/wiki/49th_G7_summit" title="49th G7 summit">49th G7 summit</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 13 March 2023, Albanese travelled to San Diego to officially commence the <a href="/wiki/AUKUS" title="AUKUS">AUKUS</a> security pact with president Biden and United Kingdom prime minister <a href="/wiki/Rishi_Sunak" title="Rishi Sunak">Rishi Sunak</a>. Through the deal, which was signed by Albanese's predecessor, Australia will procure $368 billion worth of defence materiel, including <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_submarines" class="mw-redirect" title="Nuclear submarines">nuclear-powered submarines</a> in an effort to counter China's dominance in the Indo-Pacific region.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> The deal has been negatively received by China<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> and former prime minister <a href="/wiki/Paul_Keating" title="Paul Keating">Paul Keating</a>, who called it the "worst deal in all history".<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_views">Political views</span></h2>
<p>Albanese has described his political views as <a href="/wiki/Progressivism" title="Progressivism">progressive</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> and is aligned with the <a href="/wiki/Labor_Left" title="Labor Left">Labor Left</a> faction.<sup id="cite_ref-Conversation_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conversation-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese is a <a href="/wiki/Republicanism_in_Australia" title="Republicanism in Australia">republican</a>, and supports replacing Australia's current <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Australia" title="Monarchy of Australia">constitutional monarchy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> In a debate to mark the <a href="/wiki/Platinum_Jubilee_of_Elizabeth_II#Australia" title="Platinum Jubilee of Elizabeth II">Queen's Platinum Jubilee</a>, he told the Australian Parliament, "Even many Australians who do not hold with the principle of monarchy feel regard for her. You can be a republican, as I am, and still have the deepest respect for the Queen. She has done her duty with fidelity, integrity, humanity and, as she sometimes lets slip, a sly sense of humour."<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> He has stated his desire to give <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_recognition_of_Indigenous_Australians" title="Constitutional recognition of Indigenous Australians">constitutional recognition</a> to <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_Australians" title="Indigenous Australians">Indigenous Australians</a>, and pledged to hold a referendum regarding an <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_Voice_to_Parliament" title="Indigenous Voice to Parliament">Indigenous Voice to Parliament</a> upon becoming prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> He has stated that this recognition should come before any referendum regarding Australia's status as a constitutional monarchy vs. a republic.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_issues">Social issues</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AAlbanese.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/AAlbanese.jpg/170px-AAlbanese.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/AAlbanese.jpg/255px-AAlbanese.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/AAlbanese.jpg/340px-AAlbanese.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="1280" /></a><figcaption>Albanese at the <a href="/wiki/Sydney_Gay_and_Lesbian_Mardi_Gras" title="Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras">Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras</a> in 2003</figcaption></figure>
<p>Albanese supports <a href="/wiki/Abortion_rights" class="mw-redirect" title="Abortion rights">abortion rights</a>, stating in an interview in August 2019 that he believes "women do have a right to choose".<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> He is also in favour of legalising <a href="/wiki/Voluntary_euthanasia" title="Voluntary euthanasia">voluntary euthanasia</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> in December 2022, his government repealed the <i><a href="/wiki/Euthanasia_Laws_Act_1997" title="Euthanasia Laws Act 1997">Euthanasia Laws Act 1997</a></i>, which prevented the <a href="/wiki/States_and_territories_of_Australia" title="States and territories of Australia">territories of Australia</a> from legalising euthanasia.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese is a supporter and advocate for <a href="/wiki/LGBT" title="LGBT">LGBT</a> rights,<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> and has regularly participated in the <a href="/wiki/Sydney_Gay_and_Lesbian_Mardi_Gras" title="Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras">Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras</a> since 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-Conversation_150-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conversation-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> When Labor Party members were granted a <a href="/wiki/Conscience_vote" title="Conscience vote">conscience vote</a> on the <i>Marriage Amendment Bill 2012</i>, which would have legalised same-sex marriage in Australia, Albanese voted in favour of the bill, which was unsuccessful.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> He opposed <a href="/wiki/Australian_Marriage_Law_Postal_Survey" title="Australian Marriage Law Postal Survey">holding a plebiscite for same-sex marriage</a>, stating that "we shouldn't be having a public vote where we get to judge other families".<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, Albanese also voted in favour of the <i><a href="/wiki/Marriage_Amendment_(Definition_and_Religious_Freedoms)_Act_2017" title="Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Act 2017">Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Act 2017</a></i>, the bill which ultimately legalised same-sex marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> Albanese has voiced support of transgender rights, saying that "people coming to terms with their identity and who they are, I think that they need to be respected."<sup id="cite_ref-:0_156-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Early in his political career, Albanese supported <a href="/wiki/Drug_liberalization" title="Drug liberalization">drug decriminalisation</a>, telling Parliament in 1997 that "drug use by individuals is a health issue, not a criminal issue".<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> However, in February 2022, he declined to commit to decriminalisation of <a href="/wiki/Hard_drugs" class="mw-redirect" title="Hard drugs">hard drugs</a>, commenting that the "current settings are appropriate".<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In July 2015, Albanese stated his opposition to the government's policy of turning back <a href="/wiki/Asylum_seeker" title="Asylum seeker">asylum seekers</a> who arrive to the country via boat, saying: "I couldn't ask someone else to do something that I couldn't see myself doing ... if people were in a boat including families and children, I myself couldn't turn that around."<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/2022_Australian_federal_election" title="2022 Australian federal election">2022 federal election</a> campaign, Albanese clarified that boat turnbacks would be incorporated into his government's policy,<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> leading to some critics accusing him of "flip-flopping" on the issue.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> In August 2021, after the <a href="/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive" title="2021 Taliban offensive">Taliban seized control</a> of <a href="/wiki/Afghanistan" title="Afghanistan">Afghanistan</a>, Albanese urged the <a href="/wiki/Morrison_government" title="Morrison government">Morrison government</a> to give <a href="/wiki/Afghan_refugees" title="Afghan refugees">Afghan refugees</a> permanent residency in Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_issues">Environmental issues</span></h3>
<p>While serving in the <a href="/wiki/Gillard_government" title="Gillard government">Gillard government</a>, Albanese supported the introduction of <a href="/wiki/Carbon_pricing" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon pricing">carbon pricing</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> and voted, along with the rest of the Labor Party, to establish the <i><a href="/wiki/Clean_Energy_Act_2011" title="Clean Energy Act 2011">Clean Energy Act 2011</a></i>, which instituted a <a href="/wiki/Carbon_pricing_in_Australia" title="Carbon pricing in Australia">carbon pricing scheme in Australia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Abbott_government" title="Abbott government">Abbott government</a> abolished the scheme in July 2014,<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> Albanese stated that carbon pricing was no longer needed, as "the circumstances have changed".<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese is a prominent backer of <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Australia" title="Renewable energy in Australia">renewable energy in Australia</a> and has declared that the country's "long-term future lies in renewable energy sources".<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Upon his election in 2022, he said he would "end the <a href="/wiki/Climate_wars" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate wars">climate wars</a>" and mitigation and policies to address <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Australia" title="Climate change in Australia">climate change in Australia</a> would be a priority for his government, in contrast with those preceding it.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy">Foreign policy</span></h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:20230521outreach_061.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/20230521outreach_061.jpg/252px-20230521outreach_061.jpg" decoding="async" width="252" height="183" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/20230521outreach_061.jpg/378px-20230521outreach_061.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/20230521outreach_061.jpg/504px-20230521outreach_061.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2600" data-file-height="1887" /></a><figcaption>Albanese with <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Japan" title="Prime Minister of Japan">Japanese Prime Minister</a> <a href="/wiki/Fumio_Kishida" title="Fumio Kishida">Fumio Kishida</a>, in 2023</figcaption></figure>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:NATO_Summit,_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg/252px-NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg" decoding="async" width="252" height="168" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg/378px-NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg/504px-NATO_Summit%2C_Vilnius.P061598-844404.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6124" data-file-height="4082" /></a><figcaption>Albanese with European Commission President <a href="/wiki/Ursula_von_der_Leyen" title="Ursula von der Leyen">Ursula von der Leyen</a> in <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> Summit in <a href="/wiki/Vilnius" title="Vilnius">Vilnius</a>, Lithuania, in 2023</figcaption></figure>
<p>In February 2003, he criticised the planned US-led <a href="/wiki/2003_invasion_of_Iraq" title="2003 invasion of Iraq">invasion of Iraq</a>, saying that "Whatever criticisms can be made of the Iraqi regime, Islamic fundamentalism is not one of them. This is one of the reasons the United States supported Saddam Hussein in the 1980s, including supplying his regime with weapons of mass destruction, which he then used against both the Iranians and the Kurds."<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Albanese has repeatedly urged for the release of Australian whistleblower and founder of <a href="/wiki/WikiLeaks" title="WikiLeaks">WikiLeaks</a> <a href="/wiki/Julian_Assange" title="Julian Assange">Julian Assange</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese's views on the <a href="/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict" title="Israeli–Palestinian conflict">Israeli–Palestinian conflict</a> have shifted over the years. During the <a href="/wiki/2014_Gaza_War" title="2014 Gaza War">2014 Gaza War</a>, he called Israel's bombardment of the <a href="/wiki/Gaza_Strip" title="Gaza Strip">Gaza Strip</a> a "collective punishment" that was "completely unacceptable".<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> In 2018, he challenged a decision by the Australian government to vote against a UN human rights council motion calling for an investigation into the killings of Palestinian protesters during the <a href="/wiki/2018%E2%80%932019_Gaza_border_protests" title="2018–2019 Gaza border protests">Great March of Return</a>. Shortly before the 2022 election, however, Albanese told <i><a href="/wiki/The_Australian_Jewish_News" title="The Australian Jewish News">The Australian Jewish News</a></i> that any decision he takes on Israel-Palestine will contribute "to a peaceful resolution of the conflict and to progress towards a two-state solution". When asked about a 2018 resolution to <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine" title="International recognition of the State of Palestine">recognise</a> the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Palestine" title="State of Palestine">State of Palestine</a>, Albanese insisted the motion "has no greater or lesser weight" than it did previously.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> In October 2022, his government reversed the Morrison government's decision to recognise <a href="/wiki/West_Jerusalem" title="West Jerusalem">West Jerusalem</a> as Israel's capital.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2022, Albanese condemned <a href="/wiki/2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine">Russia's invasion of Ukraine</a>, remarking in a press release that it was a "grave moment for humanity".<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 2022, Albanese said Australia's relationship with <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a> would remain "a difficult one".<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> He said that "Australia values human rights. We have spoken out about the <a href="/wiki/Uyghur_genocide" title="Uyghur genocide">treatment of Uyghurs</a>, about what's occurred in Hong Kong, about Taiwan, about other minorities including in Tibet, that are suffering from human rights abuses."<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<p>In 2000, Albanese married <a href="/wiki/Carmel_Tebbutt" title="Carmel Tebbutt">Carmel Tebbutt</a>, a future <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Premier_of_New_South_Wales" title="Deputy Premier of New South Wales">Deputy Premier of New South Wales</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> They had met in Young Labor during the late 1980s,<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> and have one son together.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> The two separated in January 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> In June 2020, it was reported that Albanese was in a relationship<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> with <a href="/wiki/Jodie_Haydon" title="Jodie Haydon">Jodie Haydon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Albanese said they had met at a dinner event in Melbourne a year after his separation from Tebbutt.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Albanese is the first divorcee to be appointed prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese describes himself as "half-Italian and half-Irish"<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> and a "non-practising Catholic".<sup id="cite_ref-catholic_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-204">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> He is also a music fan who, not long after becoming prime minister, attended a <a href="/wiki/Gang_of_Youths" title="Gang of Youths">Gang of Youths</a> concert at the <a href="/wiki/Enmore_Theatre" title="Enmore Theatre">Enmore Theatre</a><sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> and previously intervened as transport minister to save a <a href="/wiki/Dolly_Parton" title="Dolly Parton">Dolly Parton</a> tour from bureaucratic red tape.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, he co-hosted a pre-election special of music program <i><a href="/wiki/Rage_(TV_program)" title="Rage (TV program)">Rage</a></i> and his song selection included the <a href="/wiki/Pixies_(band)" title="Pixies (band)">Pixies</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Pogues" title="The Pogues">the Pogues</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Smiths" title="The Smiths">the Smiths</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Triffids" title="The Triffids">the Triffids</a>, <a href="/wiki/PJ_Harvey" title="PJ Harvey">PJ Harvey</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nirvana_(band)" title="Nirvana (band)">Nirvana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hunters_%26_Collectors" title="Hunters &amp; Collectors">Hunters &amp; Collectors</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joy_Division" title="Joy Division">Joy Division</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a lifelong supporter of the <a href="/wiki/South_Sydney_Rabbitohs" title="South Sydney Rabbitohs">South Sydney Rabbitohs</a> rugby league club, he was a board member of the club from 1999 to 2002 and influential in the fight to have the club readmitted to the <a href="/wiki/National_Rugby_League" title="National Rugby League">National Rugby League</a> (NRL) competition.<sup id="cite_ref-souths_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-souths-209">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> During October 2009, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Sydney_Morning_Herald" title="The Sydney Morning Herald">The Sydney Morning Herald</a></i> reported that Albanese had opposed an attempt to appoint the former Liberal prime minister <a href="/wiki/John_Howard" title="John Howard">John Howard</a> to a senior position in the NRL. Albanese stated he had phoned the NRL chief executive, <a href="/wiki/David_Gallop" title="David Gallop">David Gallop</a>, as well as other league officials, to advise them against the idea. He then implored officials at Souths to help stop the suggestion from gaining momentum.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, he was made a life member of the club.<sup id="cite_ref-souths_209-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-souths-209">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> He is also a fan of <a href="/wiki/Australian_rules_football" title="Australian rules football">Australian rules football</a>, and supports the <a href="/wiki/Hawthorn_Football_Club" title="Hawthorn Football Club">Hawthorn Football Club</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Albanese was injured in a <a href="/wiki/Side_collision" title="Side collision">side collision</a> while driving in <a href="/wiki/Marrickville,_New_South_Wales" title="Marrickville, New South Wales">Marrickville, New South Wales</a>, on 8 January 2021. He underwent treatment at <a href="/wiki/Royal_Prince_Alfred_Hospital" title="Royal Prince Alfred Hospital">Royal Prince Alfred Hospital</a> and was reportedly "injured externally and internally and had suffered considerable shock in the immediate aftermath of the impact". The other driver was a 17-year-old who received a ticket for negligent driving.<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> Emergency workers told Albanese that if the teen's car had hit just 30 centimetres either side of where it did, Albanese "would almost certainly have been killed".<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> Shortly following this accident, Albanese lost over 18 kilograms (39 pounds) by cutting out carbohydrates and reducing his alcohol intake, in an effort to be "match fit" for his election campaign.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite_note-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-3">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Both pronunciations have been used by Albanese himself during his life; they are both in common use among other speakers. While Albanese always used <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span></span>/</a></span></span> throughout his early life,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016240_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMiddleton2016240-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> he has more recently been heard using <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span></span>/</a></span></span>.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
